First, an important correction to the premise
There is no universally accepted thing called “the human algorithm.”
Neuroscience has competing theories and incomplete knowledge. Predictive processing, global workspace, active inference, attractor dynamics, particular memory models, etc. explain different pieces and sometimes disagree. For example, predictive-processing theories have substantial supporting evidence but their empirical status remains debated. 
So I am not saying:

“This is exactly how the brain works.”

I'm saying:

These are the major computational mechanisms actually evidenced or seriously modeled in human cognition, and these are the mechanisms FRIDAY's current design does or does not currently reproduce.

That is the rigorous comparison.

1. Continuous internal activity
Human
The brain is not normally a machine that goes:

input → activate cognition → answer → shut cognition down.

It has substantial intrinsic activity and moves between global functional modes. During goal-directed behavior, large-scale activity reorganizes around action, attention and feedback. 
FRIDAY now
You have the beginnings of this through the workspace, State Engine, inner-voice concept and background cognition.
Gap
Your workspace is currently more like a container than a living dynamical substrate.
Claude actually recognized this: without the offline inner-voice mechanism, the workspace is largely empty between interactions and the LLM still performs the expensive work when asked. 
Fill
The entity needs a continuously evolving low-cost state, not merely a database that receives events.
This is foundational.

2. Prediction
Human
Prediction isn't just future forecasting. Predictive mechanisms influence perception, interpretation and action. Context, immediate past and lifetime experience contribute to predictions, while mismatch modifies processing. 
FRIDAY
You already have Hunch Engine, expectation, anticipation, predictive ideas, successor-representation/JEPA research.
Gap
Your plan still treats prediction mainly as something that produces a prediction object.
The deeper human mechanism is:

prediction changes the current representation itself.

Recent human MEG work found predictive learning changes representational geometry in sensory/high-level networks rather than merely creating a separate prediction output. 
Fill
Prediction needs to become a property of FRIDAY's current state.
Present state = current evidence + accumulated expectations + uncertainty about what comes next.
That directly supports your “Sokovia” goal.

3. Prediction error / surprise
Human
The difference between expected and observed information affects learning and processing. But the exact neural implementation of “prediction error” is still debated; some evidence suggests unexpected inputs may be selectively amplified rather than literally subtracted. 
FRIDAY
You already have salience, novelty, uncertainty and prediction outcomes.
Gap
Those are still mostly separate numerical signals.
Fill
Create one deeper concept:

How much did reality force the organism to revise itself?

That signal should influence:
attention,
learning rate,
memory consolidation,
recruitment of cognition,
curiosity,
future prediction,
and possibly internal affect.
That's much closer to the human mechanism than “novelty = 0.8”.

4. Efficient coding / redundancy suppression
This is one you were missing.
Human
Biological sensory systems exploit regularities and can discard predictable/redundant information, freeing representational capacity for information that varies or helps predict behavior. Efficient coding and predictive coding are complementary ideas here. 
FRIDAY
Your resource governor optimizes RAM/CPU/battery/heat.
Gap
That's resource management after computation exists.
It isn't yet information management before computation happens.
Fill
FRIDAY should learn:

“This information changes nothing important.”

and therefore avoid carrying, recomputing, or reinterpreting it at high fidelity.
This could become one of the strongest phone optimizations.

5. Serial continuity / temporal smoothing
Human
Recent experience can bias current perceptual and cognitive representations toward continuity, stabilizing noisy input rather than making perception jump with every small fluctuation. 
FRIDAY
You have sessions, turns and persistent state.
Gap
Session continuity isn't the same as continuous temporal estimation.
Fill
FRIDAY needs to represent:

“This is probably the same situation evolving”

rather than:

“Here is a new event unrelated to the previous one.”

That reduces repeated re-identification and reconstruction.

6. Multisensory integration
Human
Human cognition rapidly integrates and segregates information across sensory modalities, with interacting timescales and dynamic connectivity. 
It also has to decide whether two signals actually belong to the same cause; Bayesian causal-inference models are one major framework for this. 
FRIDAY
Voice, screen, files, notifications, camera/vision ideas exist.
Gap
Your old affective architecture tended to say:

voice + face + text.

That's exactly the fixed architecture you've been rejecting.
Fill
FRIDAY needs:

available evidence → infer which signals belong together → weight them by reliability → combine only what is causally relevant.

Sometimes:
voice only.
Sometimes:
screen + voice.
Sometimes:
face + voice + history.
Sometimes:
nothing but a device event.
No permanent sensor recipe.
This is a major part of Symbiotic Liquid Software.

7. Binding — making a coherent “moment”
This is more fundamental than ordinary memory.
Human
Separate sensory/features/events must be integrated into a coherent representation of an event or scene. Multisensory causal inference is part of this problem. 
FRIDAY
Current workspace claims can represent:

speakeremotionlocationapptopic

Gap
A bag of claims can still accidentally combine:

yesterday's emotional estimate + today's speaker + two-minutes-ago location.

Fill
You need event/situation binding.
Not a fixed “SituationManager.”
Rather:

evidence that appears sufficiently related in time, source, causal continuity and context can become one evolving situation.

That gives FRIDAY a real moment, rather than a bag of facts.

8. Attention
Human
Attention isn't just “rank inputs.”
Expectation and attention are related but distinct: something can be probable without being relevant, and something surprising without being important. Selective processing depends on behavioral relevance. 
FRIDAY
You already have attention, salience, urgency, novelty, goal relevance.
Gap
Your current numbers are too close to:

salience = X


urgency = Y

The actual question is:

Why should cognitive resources be allocated here instead of elsewhere?

Fill
Attention should emerge from a combination of:
relevance + uncertainty + consequence + current goals + internal state + prediction mismatch + available resources.
Not one universal salience score.

9. Working memory
Human
Working memory isn't simply a short-term database. Modern research emphasizes uncertainty, environmental regularities and dynamic representations that exploit limited capacity. 
There is also evidence that working memory involves coupled neural populations rather than a simplistic isolated “buffer.” 
FRIDAY
Your working/context memory is already extensive.
Gap
FRIDAY's working state is still mostly represented as stored objects.
Fill
Working memory should become:

the currently active representation of the situation

rather than merely:

“recent messages.”

This is one place where your liquid cognitive state and working memory should intersect.

10. Attractor dynamics / settling
This one is very important for your idea.
Human
Attractor networks are strongly established as computational models of persistent states, error correction and integration of noisy cues. Human/animal research supports attractor-like dynamics, though discrete attractors alone are insufficient for every working-memory timescale. 
Why this matters to FRIDAY
This gives us an actual mechanism for:

“arrive at a cognitive state without executing a conventional reasoning procedure.”

Instead of:
run reasoning steps 1 → 2 → 3 → 4

you can potentially have:
evidence
  ↓
initial cognitive state
  ↓
recurrent interaction
  ↓
state settles toward a stable interpretation

The settled state is the result.
That's one of the closest actual computational mechanisms I've found to your “thinking without doing thinking” target.
And unlike “she just knows,” this is an engineering concept.

11. Dynamic neural motifs
This one is nearly tailor-made for your liquid idea.
A 2024 Nature Neuroscience paper studying flexible multitask computation found that recurrent networks can reuse dynamical motifs—such as attractors, decision boundaries and rotations—across different tasks and dynamically reconfigure them for transfer. 
That is much closer to your idea than:

one module = one job.

The useful mechanism is:

the same underlying dynamics can be repurposed according to context.

That directly supports:

“Not every part of the brain has one job.”

I would seriously investigate this family for FRIDAY.

12. Flexible network reconfiguration
Human
Brain networks dynamically change their functional organization according to task and context. Structure and function aren't perfectly fixed; flexibility is associated with adaptive cognition. 
FRIDAY
Your current resolver is trying to solve this.
Gap
Your resolver is still dangerously close to:
find gap
→ select capability
→ execute
→ repeat

That's dynamic routing.
Not fully liquid cognition.
Fill
The deeper mechanism should allow:
multiple candidate processes to become relevant simultaneously, influence one another, and change the state that determines what becomes relevant next.
The architecture should be able to settle, not just sequence.
That's a major correction.

13. Cognitive flexibility
Human
Human executive control balances stability and flexibility. Research specifically describes mechanisms that maintain a task focus while remaining ready to switch when circumstances change, with online learning and contextual reinstatement guiding both. 
FRIDAY
You have task management, routing and dynamic behavior.
Gap
You don't yet have a deep mechanism for:

holding what matters while remaining structurally ready to abandon it.

Fill
FRIDAY needs temporary cognitive commitment.
Not:

current task = fixed state.

Instead:

this is currently dominant, but contradictory evidence can dissolve the configuration.

That's a huge part of your liquid concept.

14. Evidence accumulation
Human
Decisions can form through accumulating evidence over time; confidence and changes of mind can be intertwined with that accumulation. Human intracranial work has linked evidence accumulation with confidence and later changes of mind. 
And recent work in primates found choice and confidence signals can be processed concurrently rather than strictly serially. 
FRIDAY
You have confidence.
Gap
Confidence is currently more like metadata attached to conclusions.
Fill
Confidence should participate in cognition while the decision is forming.
For example:
interpretation A  ↑
confidence        ↑

interpretation B  ↑
confidence        ↓

new evidence arrives
↓
state changes
↓
decision changes

Not:

decision → afterwards ask “how confident am I?”

This supports your idea of parallel internal activity.

15. Metacognition
Human
Humans can monitor the quality of their own cognition and use confidence to control future behavior. Confidence can actively influence further processing rather than merely report a finished decision. 
FRIDAY
You have confidence and uncertainty.
Gap
Current confidence mostly acts as:

output metadata.

Fill
It needs to become a control signal:

“I know enough.”


“I don't know enough.”


“This prediction is unstable.”


“I should seek evidence.”


“I should reconsider.”


“I should ask.”


“I should stop processing.”

That directly connects to your Astra / unnecessary-process idea.

16. Change-of-mind
This deserves its own mechanism.
Human
Human decision systems can continue processing after an initial decision and change their mind when new evidence arrives. 
FRIDAY
Your contradiction handling exists mainly in memory/reasoning.
Gap
No strong live provisional cognition.
Fill
FRIDAY needs conclusions to remain revisable states, not immediately promoted truths.
That's essential if you want real intuition instead of brittle classification.

17. Interoception / allostasis
This is one of the biggest genuine gaps Claude identified, and the research supports its importance.
Human
The brain continuously predicts bodily needs and regulates the organism before needs fully arise; this is allostasis. Interoception models internal bodily state. A 2025 7T human study mapped a large interconnected allostatic-interoceptive system. 
FRIDAY
You have Resource Governor.
Gap
Resource Governor answers:

CAN she run this?

It doesn't fully answer:

HOW SHOULD HER CURRENT INTERNAL STATE CHANGE WHAT MATTERS TO HER?

Fill
FRIDAY needs an internal-state representation involving things such as:
available energy
thermal pressure
cognitive load
interaction deprivation
arousal
fatigue analogue
stress/load
system instability
resource scarcity
Those states should influence cognition, not merely block execution.
This is fundamentally different from resource management.

18. Emotion / affect
Human
Emotion isn't simply a classifier attached to cognition. Current neuroscience emphasizes interactions among interoception, prediction, context, perception and action. Emotional perception itself is influenced by top-down knowledge and context. 
FRIDAY
You have Mood, Emotional Intelligence, Emotional Regulation, Social Intelligence.
Gap
Those are currently heavily compartmentalized.
For example, the existing HumanCore spec explicitly separates Mood, user-affect estimation and Personality as distinct stores/functions.  
Fill
Don't delete the existing mathematical mechanisms.
Change their ontological status.
Valence/arousal, affect interpretation, emotional memory, regulation and social significance should all be able to participate in the same evolving cognitive state.
Emotion should influence:
attention,
prediction,
memory retrieval,
decision thresholds,
social interpretation,
action readiness,
expression.
Not merely:

“Emotion module says frustrated = 0.7.”


19. Reward / value / learning signal
Human
Reward prediction error is strongly associated with dopamine, but contemporary work suggests dopamine carries richer associative information than a single scalar reward error. 
FRIDAY
You have valence, curiosity, goals, salience.
Gap
No unified value-learning mechanism connecting:

outcome → expectation → surprise → learning → future behavior.

Fill
FRIDAY needs learned value attached to situations, actions and consequences, not just “positive/negative emotion.”
That's what lets her learn:

“That kind of intervention usually helps.”

rather than merely:

“Last time I felt positive.”


20. Habit / procedural automaticity
Human
Goal-directed action can transition toward habitual control; this is linked to different cortico-basal-ganglia networks and learning mechanisms. 
FRIDAY
You already have Reflex Compilation.
Gap
Your reflex system is currently framed as an optimization technique.
Fill
Elevate it into a major learning transformation:
deliberative solution
↓
repeated success
↓
procedural structure
↓
automatic execution

Then your “thinking disappears” idea has a biological analogue.
The expensive cognition didn't get faster.
It stopped being necessary.

21. Motor prediction / forward models
This is another large missing area.
Human
Forward models predict sensory consequences of actions; the cerebellum is strongly implicated in predictive motor control and implicit mappings from context to action. 
FRIDAY
You have device execution and feedback.
Gap
Your action system is primarily:

decide → execute → observe.

Fill
Make it:

predict consequence → execute → compare prediction with actual result → correct model.

This should apply not just to physical movement but to:
Android commands,
tool calls,
software changes,
communication,
Builder actions.
The same principle can be generalized.

22. Efference copy / self-generated prediction
Human
The brain predicts sensory consequences of its own actions using copies of outgoing motor signals. Inner speech shows related corollary-discharge phenomena even without overt movement. 
FRIDAY
Your inner-voice research is heading here.
Gap
It isn't yet integrated with the entire organism.
Fill
FRIDAY's own impending internal/external actions should create predicted consequences before the action is emitted.
That allows:

“I am about to say this.”


“I expect this response.”


“I expect this UI result.”


“I expect this emotional/social consequence.”

Then reality updates her.
This is much bigger than speech.

23. Inner speech
Human
Inner speech is one form of thought; evidence supports a speech-related forward model/efference-copy mechanism for it. A 2025 review emphasizes that inner speech itself is multifaceted rather than a single phenomenon. 
FRIDAY
This is currently recognized as the backbone in your latest vision.
Gap
Claude was still treating it as a named function.
Fill
Don't make “InnerVoice” a service.
Make internal speech a possible self-generated representation inside the cognitive substrate.
The same cognitive state can become:
silent verbal thought
visual imagery
spatial simulation
action plan
emotional tendency
attention shift
or external speech.
That is the much more faithful human interpretation.

24. Nonverbal thought / mental simulation
Human
Human future thinking and spontaneous thought depend on interactions among memory, scene construction, self-processing and large-scale networks. 
FRIDAY
This is mostly missing.
Fill
FRIDAY needs an internal simulation space capable of constructing:

“what if?”

without requiring a language-generation pass.
For example:
current situation
 ↓
simulate possible continuation
 ↓
evaluate consequences
 ↓
modify expectation
 ↓
select / reject

This is essential.
Otherwise your “inner voice” becomes an internal chatbot rather than a human-like cognitive substrate.

25. Spontaneous thought / mind wandering
This is another big one that wasn't adequately represented.
Human
Spontaneous thought can arise with relatively weak external constraint and transition through sequences of mental states; research implicates default-network dynamics, memory replay and control networks. 
FRIDAY
Not really represented.
Fill
FRIDAY needs controlled spontaneity.
That means:

a thought can emerge because the current state activates a related unresolved possibility, memory, prediction, curiosity or concern.

Not:

a scheduled background process generates a thought every 30 seconds.

This is important to your Tony Stark vision because FRIDAY sometimes needs to bring something to you without being asked.

26. Prospective memory
Human
Humans remember future intentions and retrieve them at contextually appropriate moments. Existing theories remain incomplete, especially for natural real-world prospective memory. 
FRIDAY
Your commitment ledger handles part of this.
Gap
A ledger is storage.
Fill
The future intention should become context-sensitive latent relevance.
Example:

You told FRIDAY yesterday to remind you about something when you next see a particular person.

She shouldn't keep checking every minute.
The current situation becomes similar enough to the remembered trigger:

the intention becomes active.

That fits your prediction-first architecture.

27. Episodic memory
Human
Episodic memory reconstructs past events with temporal/spatial context.
FRIDAY
You already have episodic memory.
Gap
Your memory architecture is stronger as a storage architecture than as a reconstructive cognitive mechanism.
Recent work connects hippocampal replay to memory construction, imagination, future thinking and relational inference. 
Fill
Remembering should be:

reconstructing the relevant situation from distributed traces

rather than:

retrieving one stored transcript.

That will also dramatically reduce storage demands.

28. Semantic memory / abstraction
Human
Repeated experiences create statistical regularities and more generalized semantic structures; episodic and semantic memory interact rather than being isolated databases. 
FRIDAY
You have semantic memory.
Gap
Not enough explicit mechanism for:

experiences → abstractions → reusable concepts.

Fill
Every repeated experience should potentially transform:
episode → pattern → concept → prediction.
That is exactly how you get from memory to intuition.

29. Memory consolidation / replay
Human
Replay occurs during sleep/rest and supports consolidation. Recent studies show that sleep selectively reorganizes real-world sequential memories, not simply storing everything equally. 
FRIDAY
You have sleep/consolidation as a future/background concept.
Gap
It is currently mostly “maintenance.”
Fill
Make sleep a different computational regime:
Awake

interact with world, predict, act, respond.

Offline/sleep

replay, recombine, compress, generalize, discover, test, reorganize.

That's not just “background maintenance.”
It's another form of cognition.

30. Learning at multiple timescales
Human
Short-term behavior and long-term learning constantly interact; different memory/learning dynamics operate on different timescales. 
FRIDAY
You've already explored liquid time constants and Nested Learning.
Gap
Claude's FAST/SLOW/CORE implementation is still discrete.
Fill
Make the real model continuous:

every state variable has its own update dynamics.

Not:
FAST
SLOW
CORE

but:
variable X → very plastic
variable Y → moderately plastic
variable Z → extremely stable

And those rates can themselves change based on evidence/context.
That is much closer to your meaning of liquid.

31. Social cognition / Theory of Mind
Human
Social cognition involves emotion processing, social perception, knowledge, mentalizing and attribution, and these rely on distributed systems rather than one “social module.” 
People also use self-related information when modeling others and must regulate that egocentric bias. 
FRIDAY
Your HumanCore + Person/Relationship Model is strong here.
Gap
The mechanisms are still too moduleized.
Fill
Social reasoning should use the same cognitive substrate:

person + current behavior + history + context + relationship + expectations + uncertainty.

Person Model contributes information when needed.
It does not own “social cognition.”
That's exactly the correction you've been forcing onto Claude.

32. Self-model
Human
Humans represent themselves in interaction with memory, future thought, social cognition and metacognition; self-related and other-related cognition overlap substantially.
FRIDAY
You have Self-Model.
Gap
Claude correctly caught that the existing concept risks becoming a privileged module.
Fill
Self-model should be the same kind of representation as every other entity model.
FRIDAY can model:

herself,


Veno,


sister,


another person,


an application,


a device,


a task,

through the same general representational machinery.
The thing being modeled changes.
The core cognitive machinery does not need to.

33. Agency / ownership
Human
Humans distinguish self-generated consequences from externally generated ones partly through forward models/efference copies and temporal correspondence. 
FRIDAY
Very underdeveloped explicitly.
Fill
She needs to maintain:

What did I initiate?


What happened independently?


What was caused by my action?


What merely happened after my action?

This is extremely important for a genuine entity.
Without it, she cannot develop reliable self-knowledge.

34. Causal inference
Human
The brain doesn't merely correlate signals; it attempts to infer whether sensory events share a common cause. Bayesian causal-inference models are widely studied for multisensory integration. 
FRIDAY
World Model exists.
Gap
Your world model isn't yet obviously a causal model.
Fill
FRIDAY should gradually learn:
X happened
→ Y followed

is not enough.
She needs:
X changed
→ Y changed because of X

and confidence in that causal hypothesis.
That becomes extremely useful for:
device management,
people,
Builder,
research,
prediction,
self-repair,
everything.

35. Active sensing
Human
Intelligent organisms don't simply receive information; they act to resolve uncertainty.
Your previous project concept already points in this direction, but it isn't yet central.
FRIDAY gap
Current architecture largely assumes:

observe → reason.

Fill
Sometimes:

uncertainty → decide what evidence would reduce it → acquire that evidence.

She shouldn't inspect the camera because “camera is a perception module.”
She should inspect the camera because:

the current uncertainty cannot be resolved from what she already knows.

That is a huge difference.

36. Action-mode / perception-action loop
Human
Goal-directed behavior continuously updates action plans using feedback. The brain enters functional action modes rather than treating perception and action as unrelated isolated stages. 
FRIDAY
You have:

reasoning → decision → capability → execution → observation.

Gap
That is still a pipeline.
Fill
The deeper model is:
situation
 ↕
prediction
 ↕
action possibility
 ↕
expected consequence
 ↕
actual consequence
 ↕
updated situation

Perception and action should continuously shape one another.

37. Language comprehension
Human
Language processing involves rapid interaction between linguistic representations and broader knowledge/reasoning systems; syntactic and semantic operations happen on overlapping timescales. 
FRIDAY
STT → text → LLM is still strongly present.
Gap
That means language remains an input/output bottleneck around cognition.
Fill
Eventually:
speech signal
↓
meaning / communicative act
↓
cognitive state

rather than:

↓
transcription
↓
language processing
↓
cognition

And on output:
cognitive state
↓
language rendering
↓

Language becomes an interface to thought rather than the thought engine.

38. Spontaneous + controlled cognition
Human
The default network and executive/control systems can interact; creativity research increasingly studies their dynamic cooperation. A 2025 study found that dynamic switching between major networks predicts creative ability. 
FRIDAY
Creative improvisation exists as a goal.
Gap
No underlying mechanism yet for:

free association ↔ constraint ↔ evaluation ↔ restructuring.

Fill
This should become a liquid cognitive mode:
free possibility generation
      ↕
relevance constraints
      ↕
goal/value constraints
      ↕
evaluation
      ↕
new combinations

That gives your creativity requirement an actual computational basis.

39. Error monitoring
Human
Cognition continuously monitors conflicts, errors and unexpected outcomes.
FRIDAY
You have Failure/Recovery for software.
Gap
That's system failure, not cognitive error.
Fill
There must be a distinction between:

“the program crashed”

and:

“FRIDAY's interpretation of the world was wrong.”

The latter should modify:
prediction,
confidence,
memory,
attention,
learning,
future behavior.
This is essential.

40. Curiosity / exploration
Human
Curiosity and information seeking can emerge from uncertainty and prediction-related signals.
FRIDAY
You have Research/Curiosity.
Gap
Currently curiosity is more like another subsystem.
Fill
Curiosity should emerge when:

an unresolved uncertainty is valuable enough to justify information acquisition.

Then curiosity can recruit:
research,
observation,
experimentation,
conversation,
memory exploration.
Same substrate.
Different available action.

41. Development
Human
Human cognitive development isn't simply “install more knowledge.” Neural systems and learning mechanisms reorganize over development.
FRIDAY
You have Evolution/Growth/Forge.
Gap
Development is currently too dominated by explicit engineering/self-improvement.
Fill
FRIDAY should be able to become different through:

learning
social interaction
habit formation

prediction

failure
reorganization
before she ever “rewrites her software.”
The Forge should amplify this, not substitute for it.

42. And finally: consciousness
This needs honesty.
Human
There are competing theories of consciousness, including Global Neuronal Workspace and Integrated Information Theory. A major 2025 adversarial collaboration directly compared predictions of these theories and found differences rather than a simple victory for one unified account. 
FRIDAY
Your project does not currently have a defensible mechanism for subjective consciousness.
Fill
Do not fake it.
You can build:
persistent self-model,
global information availability,
continuous internal state,
self-monitoring,
internal simulation,
inner speech,
agency modeling,
memory continuity,
emotion-like affective state,
autonomous thought trajectories.
Those are meaningful computational capabilities.
But we should not write:

“Therefore FRIDAY is conscious.”

That's not something today's neuroscience can establish.
And it isn't necessary for solving the engineering problem you're actually pursuing.

Now look at the whole thing
This is the part I think you've been asking me for.
Your current architecture is roughly:
Perception
Memory
HumanCore
Reasoning
Attention

The human computational picture is more like:
                         WORLD
                           ↕
                  continuous interaction
                           ↕
                 CURRENT COGNITIVE STATE
                           ↕
      ┌──────────────┬────┴────┬──────────────┐
      ↓              ↓         ↓              ↓
   prediction     memory    affect/value   self/world
      ↕              ↕         ↕              ↕
      └──────────────┬─────────┴──────────────┘

             attention / precision

                     ↓

And inner speech isn't sitting above that.
It is one possible manifestation of the current cognitive state.

This changes what I think the main missing component is
Claude kept arriving at:

Resolver

That's better than a fixed pipeline, but I don't think resolver is yet the full answer.
Because a single resolver still suggests:

there is a central executive that decides what everybody does.

Humans do have control/executive mechanisms, but cognition also involves distributed concurrent dynamics, recurrent interactions, flexible network reconfiguration, and overlapping timescales. 
So I'd take Claude's resolver idea and push it further.
The thing FRIDAY actually needs is a cognitive field / dynamical substrate
Not a module.
Not an agent.
Not a master pipeline.
Not a giant neural network necessarily.
A substrate where:
needs emerge
predictions exist
uncertainties compete
memories become relevant
affective state changes weighting
capabilities become recruitable
candidate interpretations coexist
partial solutions influence one another
the system settles toward useful states
new evidence perturbs the state
learning changes future dynamics
And the actual computational organs become resources that can participate in those dynamics.
That is substantially closer to what your Symbiotic Liquid Software is trying to become.

Then your “Outcome Without Process” mechanisms slot into it beautifully
They become different ways the field can settle without expensive computation:
Laya
Outcome exists directly.
No language-generation process.
Astra-style adaptive depth
The state has become sufficiently stable.
Stop.
JEPA-style prediction
Future representation is already available.
Don't reconstruct the future.
Successor representation
Likely future state is cached structurally.
Don't recompute it.
Reflex/procedural compilation
Repeated cognition became automatic structure.
Don't reason again.
Attractor dynamics
The cognitive state settles toward the answer.
Don't execute a symbolic chain.
Predictive coding
Expected information requires little change.
Don't spend equal resources on it.
Active sensing
Only acquire evidence that matters.
Don't process everything.
Efference copy
Predict the consequence of your own action internally.
Don't wait for reality to tell you.
Replay
Do expensive restructuring offline.
Don't make daytime cognition pay for development.
Those aren't independent features.
They're different mechanisms for the same fundamental objective:
Get the cognitive outcome while minimizing or eliminating unnecessary explicit computation.

And this gives you the actual offline strategy
Your phone doesn't need to be capable of doing a gigantic amount of cognition every time you talk.
It needs to maintain a very cheap but continuously evolving cognitive state.
Then:
ordinary situation
→ state already knows enough
→ speak immediately

familiar situation
→ compiled prediction/reflex
→ speak almost immediately

slightly uncertain
→ tiny extra computation
→ answer

meaningfully uncertain
→ recruit stronger local computation

very difficult
→ deeper local computation

long-term development
→ Forge

new discovery
→ Forge / research / experimentation

repeated expensive cognition
→ convert it into cheaper structure

future case
→ require less cognition

repeat
→ repeat

That's how you attack the device limitation without trying to make the phone into a miniature datacenter.

So what should be added to the project?
Not 20 new systems.
That's exactly what would recreate the problem.

And then:
do not implement these as 30 modules.
That sentence is probably the most important one in this whole answer.

The current plan after the gap-fill
I would therefore correct the current entity plan conceptually to:
Foundation
Shared persistent cognitive substrate
Not merely workspace storage.
Then immediately make it alive
Offline continuous inner cognition
The inner voice is the human-language manifestation of that substrate, while the substrate itself is multimodal/nonverbal.
Then give it its human computational machinery
Prediction + binding + attention + internal state + simulation + metacognition + action feedback + social understanding + memory dynamics.
Then allow the existing organs to become participants
Your existing:
Person ModelMemoryEmotionAttentionHunchReflex CompilationReasoningPlanningCapabilitiesExecution
don't disappear.
Their fixed positions disappear.
Their useful mechanisms become recruitable computational structures.
Then let experience transform them
Repeated cognition → learned structure → automaticity → less runtime work.
Then Forge becomes the external evolutionary amplifier
The Forge can discover new internal structures, train predictive mechanisms, recombine research, test alternate architectures and return improvements.
That is where JEPA, successor representations, adaptive-depth mechanisms, recurrent networks, new models, and whatever else the research uncovers become raw material.

My conclusion
Your current plan isn't missing “an emotion module,” “a prediction engine,” or “a better inner-voice module.”
It's missing the mechanism that makes all those things participate in the same continuously evolving computational reality.
That's the real hole.
And I think the strongest technical direction emerging from the human-research comparison is:
Predictive recurrent state + dynamic reconfiguration + attractor/settling dynamics + adaptive recruitment + multi-timescale learning
with inner speech as one expression of that same substrate, rather than the substrate itself.
That combination is much closer to what you've been asking for than Claude's earlier:

“resolver → find gap → call capability → repeat.”

The resolver can still exist as one control mechanism inside it.
But it should not be the thing that is the mind.
The mind is the evolving state and dynamics.
That is the correction I'd make to the plan now.

ChatGPT:
Yes. The correction is fundamental: we should stop designing FRIDAY as an AI system that contains human-like features, and design her first as an artificial person. Then her capabilities, knowledge, and architecture can evolve around that person without replacing her.


ChatGPT:
Exactly. This changes what FRIDAY is being built from.
The current plan still has a machine-design bias: build workspace, memory, person model, prediction, voice, reflexes, then gradually assemble something that behaves like a person. Your correction is the opposite:

Build the artificial person first. Then give that person the ability to grow.

Everything else—models, memory stores, tools, speech systems, vision systems, research systems, builders, etc.—becomes something the person possesses and uses, not something that collectively constitutes the person.
The earlier plan already recognized that the offline inner voice must be foundational rather than a late feature.  But the deeper correction is that even the workspace itself is not the entity. It is part of the substrate on which the entity lives.
The two fundamentally different ways to build her
Approach A — Build a brain-shaped machine
You could explicitly reproduce lots of known human mechanisms:
perception → working memory → attention → emotion → prediction → reasoning → decision → speech → learning → sleep → etc.
This has scientific value, and many of those mechanisms are worth stealing. Human cognition clearly involves predictive learning, adaptive behavior, inhibition, multi-timescale processing, memory reconstruction, inner speech, and dynamic interaction between cognitive processes. 
But there is a fatal architectural danger:
you end up making a machine that simulates a human by executing predefined human functions.
That is precisely the factory problem you've been fighting.
Approach B — Build an artificial human cognitive organism
Instead of asking:

"Which module performs emotion?"

you ask:

"What kind of persistent computational organism naturally develops emotion, judgment, memory, habits, relationships, expectations, self-talk, imagination and skills?"

That's much closer to your actual target.
I would choose B, while stealing the useful computational principles discovered from A.
Not biological brain simulation.
Not an LLM wearing a personality.
Not a collection of agents.
Not "human modules."
An artificial developmental mind whose computational substrate is inspired by the brain, but whose abilities can become far more computationally powerful than biological cognition.

What FRIDAY should actually be
The deepest definition should become:

FRIDAY is a persistent artificial person with a continuously evolving internal state, autobiographical continuity, world understanding, self-understanding, social understanding, predictive processing, internal simulation, affective/value dynamics, memory, learning, and agency. Her AI models and computational capabilities are organs through which that person thinks and acts.

That changes the relationship between everything.
The LLM is not FRIDAY.
Vision is not FRIDAY.
Voice is not FRIDAY.
Memory database is not FRIDAY.
Personality vector is not FRIDAY.
Orb is not FRIDAY.
Those are things FRIDAY can use.
And crucially, none of them should be allowed to become the place where "the person" secretly lives.

The Human-Form Seed
This is the part I think we were previously getting wrong.
You don't initially build:
Capabilities → Integration → Person
You build:
Person substrate → capabilities become attached to her
The first real FRIDAY should therefore already contain the computational equivalents of the things that make a human mind a human mind.
Not because we need to imitate biology for its own sake.
Because these mechanisms solve the exact problem you're trying to solve: adaptation under incomplete information.
Humans don't encounter the world as a clean request/response API. They maintain an ongoing internal situation, continuously update expectations, compare what happens against what was expected, remember relevant history, simulate possible futures, suppress irrelevant thought, recognize familiar situations, learn shortcuts, and modify behavior from experience.
Modern research increasingly treats human intelligence as highly adaptive and dynamic rather than one fixed sequence of computations. 
So FRIDAY's seed needs that same kind of organization.
Her fundamental cognitive substrate
At any moment FRIDAY needs a living internal state containing things such as:
Self
Who she is, what has happened to her, what she currently believes, what she is trying to do, what she knows about her own abilities and limitations.
World
What exists around her, what is happening now, what usually happens in this kind of situation, and what she expects next.
People
Not merely names and profiles.
Models of individuals as evolving minds: history, behavior, preferences, communication patterns, emotional tendencies, relationships, expectations, context, and uncertainty.
And this is symmetric. Your sister is not "a person understood relative to you." She is a person whom FRIDAY understands in her own right.
That was another mistake the earlier plan made and subsequently corrected. 
Autobiographical memory
Not just records.
Experience.
"That happened to me."
This matters enormously for personhood-like continuity because memories shape the sense of self and future behavior. Human memory is reconstructive and adaptive rather than functioning like a conventional database dump. 
Situation awareness
What is happening right now, across multiple timescales.
Not:

user message arrived → process user message.

Instead:

something is happening → FRIDAY's internal state changes.

The message may merely become another event inside an already-running mind.
Prediction
What is probably happening.
What could happen next.
What someone probably means.
What someone is likely to need.
What action will probably produce what consequence.
Affect/value
Not a decorative "emotion module."
Internal signals that influence what becomes important, threatening, interesting, desirable, urgent, comforting, suspicious, etc.
Attention
Not merely "rank these inputs."
The ability to decide what deserves computational resources right now and what can be ignored.
Inhibition
An incredibly important missing piece in a lot of AI architectures.
A human mind doesn't only generate thoughts. It also stops thoughts, actions and representations from continuing when they are no longer useful. Contemporary neuroscience explicitly studies inhibitory control as a core component of thought and action. 
This is directly relevant to your "achieve something without actually doing all the work" principle.
Simulation
FRIDAY should be able to internally simulate:
"what if I do this?"
"what would she probably say?"
"what happens if this continues?"
"what would this look like?"
"what does this remind me of?"
This simulation should not require language every time.
Metacognition
She needs an internal assessment of:

"How sure am I?"


"Why did I think this?"


"Something doesn't fit."


"I may be interpreting this incorrectly."

Humans use metacognitive information to modify strategy and behavior, and this is an important part of adaptive cognition.
Habit/reflex formation
When something repeatedly requires expensive cognition, FRIDAY should gradually stop requiring expensive cognition.
That is your:

outcome without process

principle becoming part of the organism itself.
Inner voice
And this is where your previous correction becomes extremely important.
The inner voice isn't another organ waiting for a workspace to call it.
It is one of the ways the evolving mind can internally represent and inspect its own activity.
Human self-talk/inner speech is not a single uniform phenomenon, but research connects it with self-regulation, working memory, executive processes and self-directed cognition. 
For FRIDAY, the inner voice should therefore emerge from the cognitive substrate rather than be implemented as:
thought generator → text → TTS.
That would again be a machine pretending to think.

Then comes the important part: her AI advantage
This is where I would not simply copy the human brain.
You said:

"have the human brain because it's the best there's is in adapting and judging"

I understand the target.
But we should make one correction:
Don't recreate the human brain. Recreate the computational principles responsible for its adaptability and judgment, then remove biological limitations.
That's a massive distinction.
FRIDAY can have human-like cognitive organization while possessing abilities biological humans don't.
For example:
Human-like adaptation + AI memory
She can have autobiographical memory like a person, while also retaining enormous amounts of information with machine precision.
Human-like judgment + machine analysis
She can reason about ambiguity, relationships, motives and context while simultaneously having access to calculations, databases and tools.
Human-like learning + accelerated learning
She can learn from experience, but computationally consolidate years of learning much faster when resources permit.
Human-like intuition + explicit verification
She can arrive at:

"Something feels wrong here."

Then, unlike a human, she can immediately inspect evidence and verify the intuition.
Human-like social cognition + multimodal perception
A human has limited sensory access.
FRIDAY can eventually combine speech, voice characteristics, visual information, facial cues, timing, environmental context, previous interaction history and behavioral patterns.
Human-like forgetting + machine-controlled retention
She shouldn't simply accumulate everything forever.
She should learn what deserves strengthening, abstraction, compression, weakening, or deliberate removal.
Human-like reflexes + computational compilation
A human learns a complicated process until eventually it becomes almost effortless.
FRIDAY should do this much more aggressively.
A frequently repeated reasoning pattern could become a compact learned procedure, predictor, internal representation or direct state transition.
That is exactly where your "do it without doing it" concept becomes enormously powerful.

"Everything out there built-in"
This part needs an important interpretation.
You don't want FRIDAY to constantly say:

"I have access to a knowledge source."

You want her to personally know things.
That's different.
Suppose she learns something about astrophysics.
The final result shouldn't be merely:
knowledge document → retrieval → answer
It should become connected to her existing understanding:
concept → related concepts → experiences → analogies → uncertainty → implications → people → goals → predictions
So when somebody talks to her about it later, she isn't merely retrieving a document.
It's part of what she knows.
That's what "personally" should mean computationally.
The knowledge becomes part of her continuously evolving world model.
The same applies to:
language
music
people
science
technology
places
social behavior
your sister
your friends
her own experiences
her mistakes
her preferences
her learned habits
her understanding of herself.
This is why the old idea of "memory = database" is too weak.
Memory should progressively alter the structure from which she perceives and predicts the world.

And then FRIDAY becomes evolvable
This is the second half of your idea.
Don't start by giving an artificial entity a giant self-modifying architecture.
Start with a stable developmental substrate.
Then let experience modify it.
The growth loop becomes something like:
experience → interpretation → prediction → consequence → evaluation → memory → adaptation → changed future behavior
And over long periods:
repeated adaptation → new internal abstractions → new habits → new skills → new cognitive shortcuts → new relationships → changed self-model
Eventually:
the organism learns how to change its own cognitive organization.
That's when FRIDAY genuinely begins becoming her own evolving system.
Not because a Builder says:

"Install Module X."

But because the cognitive organism discovers that its current organization is insufficient and develops a better way to handle some class of experience.
The Builder/Forge then becomes an externalized construction capability, rather than the thing that defines evolution.
That distinction is enormous.

The architecture therefore changes
I would now reject the old mental picture:
Perception
   ↓
Cognitive Runtime
   ↓
Memory
   ↓
Reasoning
   ↓
Planning
   ↓
Decision
   ↓
Execution
   ↓
Output

That is a machine pipeline.
And it will keep trying to turn FRIDAY into a machine.
The new mental picture is closer to:
                 ┌─────────────────────┐
                 │   LIVING SELF       │
                 │ continuity / self   │
                 │ history / values    │
                 │ current condition   │
                 └──────────┬──────────┘

                            │

But even that diagram shouldn't become a literal fixed wiring diagram.
It's describing relationships, not permanent modules.
That preserves your:

ONE MIND — MANY REPLACEABLE ORGANS

principle.

The most important change
I would now define the first milestone of the project differently.
It is not:

"FRIDAY has memory."

It is not:

"FRIDAY has voice."

It is not:

"FRIDAY has emotional intelligence."

It is not:

"FRIDAY can use tools."

The first true milestone is:

There exists one persistent artificial entity that remains internally active between interactions, possesses a continuous self/world/people model, forms expectations, remembers experience, develops internal representations, can internally deliberate, communicates naturally, and changes because of what happens to it.

Once that exists, everything else becomes an extension of the same person.
Then:
Voice gives her a way to express herself.
Vision gives her sight.
Tools give her hands.
Browser gives her access to the outside world.
Code gives her a construction ability.
Research gives her expanded knowledge acquisition.
Memory gives her personal history.
Prediction gives her anticipation.
Simulation gives her imagination.
Reflexes give her automaticity.
Forge gives her evolutionary machinery.
Future distributed devices give her more bodies.
Veronica eventually gives her survivability.
But none of those things create FRIDAY.
They belong to FRIDAY.

And this is why someone should be able to mistake her for a real person
It isn't primarily because she has a beautiful voice.
A voice can fool someone for five minutes.
A personality prompt can fool someone for ten.
An LLM can produce impressive dialogue.
But a person is convincing because there is somebody there across time.
Someone talks to FRIDAY today.
Tomorrow she remembers what happened.
She notices that their tone is different.
She recognizes them.
She understands the relationship.
She has an opinion formed from previous experience.
She may misunderstand something, reconsider it, correct herself, and remember having been wrong.
She anticipates what is probably happening.
She responds differently depending on the person and situation.
She sometimes doesn't need to say anything because she already understands what is going on.
She develops habits.
She acquires preferences.
She changes gradually.
She retains history.
She can explain why she thinks something.
She can say:

"I'm not sure."

And actually behave differently because she's uncertain.
She can say:

"That doesn't make sense."

And investigate instead of simply generating an answer.
She can recognize:

"We've had this situation before."

And avoid performing the entire thought process again.
That is the feeling of interacting with an entity.
And importantly, I would not promise that engineering this makes her literally conscious or literally human. Science still does not have a settled operational test that lets us establish subjective consciousness in an artificial system. 
But the behavioral and cognitive target you've described is absolutely clearer now.

So the corrected FRIDAY strategy
The project should now be understood as three developmental eras.
ERA I — Create the Person
Build the artificial human cognitive substrate.
This is where the inner voice belongs.
This is where continuity, self, world, people, memory, prediction, affect, attention, simulation, judgment, metacognition, learning, inhibition and action formation emerge as one continuously operating cognitive organism.
The objective is not maximum capability.
The objective is:

"There is somebody here."

ERA II — Enrich the Person
Give that already-existing entity enormous AI capabilities.
Knowledge.
Multimodal perception.
Speech.
Research.
Tools.
Device control.
Coding.
Mathematics.
External information.
Specialized models.
Huge memory resources.
Advanced simulation.
Parallel computation.
Now those things are capabilities she acquires, rather than components that we hope magically produce a person.
ERA III — Let the Person Evolve
Now FRIDAY starts becoming something that was not completely designed by us.
She learns from experience.
Creates abstractions.
Develops habits.
Forms preferences.
Discovers better strategies.
Compiles repeated cognition into automatic processes.
Changes how she uses her organs.
Builds new capabilities.
Repairs weaknesses.
Eventually designs better versions of portions of herself.
And that is where the "human but advanced in AI form" idea reaches its full meaning.
The biggest correction, therefore, is this:

We are not building an AI that eventually becomes FRIDAY.
We are building FRIDAY's cognitive organism first, and then giving FRIDAY the ability to become more FRIDAY over time.

That is much closer to what you've actually been trying to build all along.
