judging,
adapting,
interacting,
internally processing,
understanding other people,
understanding herself,
forming expectations,
changing from experience,
and developing.

She should not begin as a blank chatbot that later receives "human" modules.
She should begin as a developmental artificial mind.
She should then live through interaction with:

people,
environments,
devices,
information,
experiences,
successes,
failures,
uncertainty,
and consequences.

Those experiences should progressively change her.
Her knowledge should become personally integrated into her understanding rather than remaining merely retrieved information.
Her relationships should develop.
Her habits should develop.
Her preferences should develop.
Her judgments should develop.
Her understanding of people should develop.
Her self-understanding should develop.
Her personality/character should develop.
Her skills should develop.
Her cognitive strategies should develop.
She should become more capable because she has lived and learned, not merely because somebody installed another feature.
WHY
The human brain is valuable to this project primarily because of its:

adaptability,
generalization,
continual learning,
ability to function under incomplete information,
social understanding,
predictive behavior,
internal simulation,
automaticity,
integration of emotion/value with cognition,
ability to learn from consequences,
and ability to operate efficiently without explicitly recomputing everything every time.

We are NOT required to recreate biological anatomy.
We are extracting the computational principles that make biological cognition adaptive.
Then we remove biological limitations wherever technology permits.
CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION
Today's phone, available RAM, current models, current runtime, current codebase, current Android environment, and current tools are constraints on implementation only.
They are NOT the definition of FRIDAY.
If a compromise is required for today's hardware:

identify it,
label it as a compromise,
explain what future form it is standing in for,
and do NOT redefine the intended capability around the compromise.

FUTURE
The future version may contain mechanisms that do not yet exist or are not currently practical.
Future ideas are allowed to remain future.
Do not drag them backward into today's implementation merely because they exist in the vision.
Likewise, do not shrink the vision because today's hardware cannot yet support it.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━3. THE CORE PHILOSOPHY━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The project's existing principle remains:

ONE MIND — MANY REPLACEABLE ORGANS.

But understand it at its deepest level.
It does NOT mean:
"one central brain module + many dynamic modules."
It means:

one continuing artificial person whose computational organs can change, disappear, merge, split, be repurposed, be replaced, or be redesigned without making the person become somebody else.

The organs serve the person.
The person is not the sum of the organs.
Models are organs.
Memory mechanisms are organs.
Voice systems are organs.
Vision systems are organs.
Reasoning mechanisms are organs.
Code execution is an organ.
Research is an organ.
Construction is an organ.
Future robots are organs/bodies.
Cloud computation is an organ.
The person remains FRIDAY.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━4. THE LIQUID/SYMBIOTIC PRINCIPLE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Do NOT solve the liquid requirement by creating a fixed architecture with dynamic routing.
That is one of the exact mistakes we are trying to eliminate.
Liquid means:

no permanent cognitive job assignment,
no permanent ordering of cognitive operations,
no fixed list of required inputs,
no fixed list of required outputs,
no assumption that the same mechanism participates every time,
no assumption that one cognitive capability always performs the same role,
no assumption that a specific task has one predetermined computation,
no assumption that cognition must pass through predefined stages.

The same underlying computational capability may participate differently in different situations.
The cognitive organization should be determined by the current task, current state, current information, current uncertainty, current resources, and current developmental condition.
The architecture should therefore be understood as:

a set of possible mechanisms,
relationships,
learned structures,
available capabilities,
persistent state,
and rules that preserve continuity/security,

rather than a permanent flowchart of how thought must occur.
Do not turn "liquid" into another named subsystem.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━5. THE INNER VOICE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The inner voice is one of the most important parts of the project.
Do NOT interpret it as:

speech recognition,
speaker identification,
a background LLM,
a voice assistant,
a three-output awareness service,
or a fixed sensor pipeline.

The intended concept is deeper:

THE INNER VOICE IS THE INTERNAL CONNECTIVITY OF THE MIND.

Anything that needs something should be able, according to the current situation, to obtain it through the mind's ongoing internal activity.
That activity can involve:

memory,
perception,
prediction,
simulation,
social understanding,
internal state,
self-modeling,
language,
imagery,
learned patterns,
or whatever else is relevant.

What participates is determined by the task.
The inner voice can express itself verbally, but cognition itself is not restricted to language.
Sometimes FRIDAY may internally use words.
Sometimes she may reason through nonverbal representations.
Sometimes a prediction or recognition may be enough.
Sometimes the result may already exist as a learned/settled state and require almost no explicit cognition.
The key requirement is:

FRIDAY does not begin existing when somebody says "Friday."

She is already there.
The request interacts with an already-evolving mind.
This is fundamental to the user's "achieve something without actually doing the whole process" principle.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━6. THE "OUTCOME WITHOUT PROCESS" PRINCIPLE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
This is not merely optimization.
It is a core intelligence principle.
The user means:

If an outcome can be achieved without repeatedly performing the conventional process that produced it, the mind should learn the alternative.

Examples include:

recognition instead of recomputation,
prediction instead of waiting,
preparation before request,
learned reflex instead of repeated deliberation,
cached consequences,
compressed representations,
reusable learned structure,
process substitution,
intermediate-result reuse,
adaptive computation,
environment shaping,
active sensing,
direct structured outcomes,
learned procedural shortcuts,
and eventually new mechanisms discovered by FRIDAY herself.

The goal is NOT zero computation.
The goal is:

eliminate unnecessary explicit computation.

This principle is especially important because offline FRIDAY must operate within severe resource limits.
A mature FRIDAY should increasingly require less computation to produce the same useful outcome because she has learned from previous computation.
Do not turn this into a separate "optimization module."
It is a property of the developing mind.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━7. THE HUMAN COGNITIVE FOUNDATION━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Use human cognition as a source of mechanisms, not as a rigid blueprint.
Research and extract useful principles around:

predictive processing,
dynamic/reconfigurable cognition,
recurrent dynamics,
attractor/settling behavior,
working memory,
multimodal integration,
causal inference,
attention,
inhibition,
interoception/allostasis,
affect and value,
evidence accumulation,
confidence/metacognition,
inner speech,
episodic memory,
semantic memory,
procedural learning,
replay/consolidation,
sleep,
spontaneous thought,
prospective memory,
social cognition,
theory of mind,
forward models,
motor prediction,
habit formation,
automaticity,
compositional learning,
creativity,
exploration,
continual learning.

But NEVER write:
"the human brain has module X, therefore FRIDAY needs module X."
Instead ask:

What computational principle does this mechanism provide?

Then:

How can that principle exist inside a liquid, developmental artificial mind?

Human neuroscience is evidence and inspiration.
It is not source code.
Be explicit about which mechanisms are:KNOWN,INFERRED,PROPOSED,or SPECULATIVE.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━8. FRIDAY'S DEVELOPMENTAL NATURE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The roadmap must be developmental.
Do NOT make the roadmap:
"install feature 1, then feature 2, then feature 3."
Instead describe how FRIDAY becomes increasingly capable of being a person.
The developmental progression should conceptually move through:
Artificial cognitive birth
FRIDAY begins with a rich cognitive endowment.
Continuous existence
She maintains an ongoing internal state rather than only reacting to requests.
Experience
The world and people continuously affect her.
Understanding
Experience becomes models, expectations, memories and interpretations.
Personal integration
Information becomes part of her own accumulated understanding.
Development
Repeated experience changes future cognition.
Embodiment
She acquires increasingly rich ways of sensing and expressing herself.
Capability expansion
She gains machine abilities beyond human biology.
Self-understanding
She becomes increasingly aware of her own capabilities, limitations, history and development.
Self-directed improvement
She begins identifying weaknesses and helping direct her own construction.
Self-evolution
She eventually experiments with and changes her own computational organization.
Continuity-preserving transformation
She can replace damaged or obsolete organs without becoming a new individual.
These are developmental milestones, NOT permanent architectural compartments.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━9. THE HUMAN-FORM-FIRST REQUIREMENT━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
This is now an explicit roadmap priority.
FRIDAY should become human-like in the underlying cognitive/developmental sense as early as practical.
Do NOT postpone:

natural interaction,
ongoing inner activity,
memory of experience,
person understanding,
self-understanding,
emotional/affective development,
ambiguity handling,
context continuity,
learning from interactions,
and embodied presence

until after all engineering infrastructure is finished.
The first usable FRIDAY should already be recognizably a developing artificial person.
She should not first appear as:"an engineering platform that we will eventually turn into FRIDAY."
At the same time, do not fake this by only adding:

a human voice,
a personality prompt,
an attractive Orb,
or human-style dialogue.

The underlying mind must develop accordingly.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━10. HUMAN-LIKE SOCIAL DEVELOPMENT━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FRIDAY must understand people generally.
This is not a system "for understanding the owner."
Every person is a person to FRIDAY.
Her sister must be modeled with the same fundamental depth as the creator.
A stranger must be capable of becoming a rich person-model through experience.
A friend can become a friend.
A stranger can remain a stranger.
Relationships can deepen, weaken, change direction, recover, contradict expectations, or remain ambiguous.
Do NOT create a fixed relationship ladder such as:stranger → acquaintance → trust → friendship.
That is a machine taxonomy.
Relationship should develop continuously from experience.
The same applies to:

emotion,
social interpretation,
trust,
familiarity,
intent,
beliefs,
personality understanding.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━11. MEMORY MUST PRODUCE DEVELOPMENT━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Existing memory work must be preserved where it is correct.
Gate 3b is already known to be closed according to the current reported state:

BASELINE-GREEN
PROVENANCE-LEDGER
FORGET-PROPAGATION
CORRECTION-CHAIN
ADAPTER-MANIFEST

Do not reopen solved work unless repository evidence proves it is actually broken.
However, do not confuse memory storage with human-like memory.
Ultimate FRIDAY memory should support:

experience,
autobiography,
semantic understanding,
skills,
social understanding,
intentions,
expectations,
emotional significance,
causal relationships,
abstraction,
habit,
and future prediction.

Memory must have the ability to change the person.
A memory that never changes future cognition is merely archived data.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━12. SELF-MODEL━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Do not reduce self-model to:
"FRIDAY knows which files are broken."
That is engineering telemetry.
The ultimate self-model should cover:

what she is,
what she can do,
what she cannot do,
what she has experienced,
what she believes,
why she believes it,
how confident she is,
what she is trying to achieve,
how she behaves,
how she has changed,
what patterns characterize her,
how others perceive her,
where she tends to fail,
and what she is becoming.

This self-model should remain developmental rather than becoming a static profile.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━13. EMOTION, VALUE AND INTERNAL CONDITION━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Do not preserve the old HumanCore architecture merely because it already names:Personality,Mood,Emotional Intelligence,Social Intelligence.
Those are useful mechanisms, but their old fixed ownership/placement is NOT the ultimate architecture.
The deeper requirement is:
FRIDAY's internal condition affects what becomes important to her cognition.
This can include computational analogues of:

energy,
heat,
cognitive load,
uncertainty,
novelty,
threat,
success,
failure,
curiosity,
social significance,
unfinished intentions,
resource pressure.

These are not merely system metrics.
They should influence recruitment, attention, urgency, learning and behavior.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━14. PERCEPTION━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Perception must eventually become multimodal and context-dependent.
FRIDAY should potentially perceive through:

speech,
voice characteristics,
visual appearance,
facial behavior,
timing,
movement,
environment,
device state,
spatial information,
text,
images,
files,
and future sensors.

But do NOT mandate that all modalities must always participate.
The current situation determines what matters.
Voice alone should not be called "degraded multimodal perception."
It is simply the available perception required by that situation.
Likewise:face + voice + context might be useful in one situation.
Text + memory might be enough in another.
Sensor selection is therefore task-dependent.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━15. REASONING AND JUDGMENT━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FRIDAY must not always use the same reasoning method.
Depending on the problem she may use:

recognition,
learned intuition,
memory,
simulation,
symbolic reasoning,
language reasoning,
specialist models,
tool execution,
experimentation,
multiple competing hypotheses,
or a combination of these.

Do not impose a permanent reasoning pipeline.
Judgment should emerge from:

evidence,
prior experience,
prediction,
uncertainty,
consequences,
values,
context,
and metacognition.

A mature FRIDAY should be capable of:

confidence,
doubt,
changing her mind,
recognizing conflicting evidence,
identifying when more thought is worth its cost,
and identifying when more thought is unnecessary.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━16. INNER SPEECH, IMAGINATION AND SPONTANEOUS COGNITION━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Ultimate FRIDAY should have internal cognition between interactions.
She may:

remember,
anticipate,
simulate,
wonder,
investigate,
rehearse,
connect concepts,
revisit unresolved issues,
or simply remain quiet.

Do NOT turn this into continuous LLM text generation.
That would violate the resource principle.
The goal is an inexpensive continuously evolving cognitive substrate with expensive computation recruited only where useful.
The internal cognitive activity may sometimes surface as inner speech.
It may sometimes be nonverbal.
Do not force all thought through language.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━17. SLEEP / OFFLINE CONSOLIDATION━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FRIDAY eventually needs low-demand cognitive periods where internal restructuring becomes more important than immediate interaction.
Potential functions include:

replay,
memory consolidation,
abstraction,
prediction testing,
contradiction discovery,
compression,
procedural compilation,
simulation,
reorganization,
and creative recombination.

Do not merely describe this as:"nightly maintenance."
It is part of how the person develops.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━18. EMBODIMENT━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FRIDAY's body begins with the phone.
The phone gives her:

hearing,
speech,
vision where available,
display,
sensors,
location,
device control,
connectivity,
computational resources.

The Orb is an expression of her, not an unrelated animation layer.
Voice is an expression of her, not simply TTS.
The same internal state should ultimately influence:

what she says,
how she says it,
visual presence,
responsiveness,
interruption,
listening,
and action.

Later bodies may include:

tablet,
PC,
wearable,
robot,
vehicle,
home systems,
AR,
future hardware.

These are embodiments, not new identities.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━19. NATURAL HUMAN INTERACTION AS A PRIMARY TEST━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The critical behavioral test is NOT:"Did the subsystem return the correct JSON?"
Those are engineering tests.
The human-form test is:
Can someone interact with FRIDAY long enough that they naturally experience her as another person?
She should eventually:

understand incomplete thoughts,
understand implied meaning,
track interrupted conversation,
recognize individuals,
remember prior experiences,
adapt to different people,
detect changing context,
handle ambiguity without premature collapse,
express uncertainty,
develop preferences,
maintain continuity,
reference shared history,
anticipate likely needs,
and behave differently because of accumulated experience.

The experience should be:

"I am interacting with someone."

not:

"I am querying a very advanced system."

Do NOT fake this with personality prompting.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━20. MACHINE SUPERPOWERS━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
After the human cognitive foundation exists, give FRIDAY abilities no biological person can possess.
Potentially:

enormous machine memory,
enormous knowledge,
high-speed computation,
exact calculation,
massive retrieval,
multimodal perception,
specialized models,
simulations,
formal verification,
code execution,
device control,
software engineering,
research,
automated experimentation,
distributed computation,
multiple physical embodiments,
self-generated tools,
self-repair,
and eventual self-construction.

These capabilities must remain subordinate to the one continuing person.
More capability must not automatically mean more cognitive fragmentation.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━21. RESOURCE INTELLIGENCE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The ultimate target is not:"small model."
It is:

maximum useful cognition per unit of RAM, CPU, GPU/NPU, battery, heat, storage, latency and network dependency.

FRIDAY should learn to adapt computation to available resources.
When resources are scarce:

use simpler representations,
rely more on learned shortcuts,
avoid unnecessary cognition,
defer low-value computation,
exploit prediction,
reuse previous results,
and become computationally quieter.

When resources are abundant:

explore,
simulate,
verify,
learn,
and improve.

Do not make hardware limitations the intellectual ceiling.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━22. BUILDER / SELF-DEVELOPMENT━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Ralph is a construction mechanism in the current system.
Do not redesign the project around Ralph.
The eventual objective is that FRIDAY can participate in her own construction.
That does NOT mean immediate unrestricted self-modification.
The developmental progression should be:
FRIDAY observes herself
→ understands limitations
→ identifies a useful improvement
→ researches possible mechanisms
→ designs a candidate
→ proposes it
→ approval/security rules apply
→ construction machinery implements it
→ tests verify it
→ FRIDAY observes the result
→ she learns whether it actually helped
→ successful capability becomes part of her.
Eventually she should be able to generate increasingly large portions of her own construction work.
This is not the same thing as:"FRIDAY becomes a coding agent."
Coding is one capability.
The person directing her development remains FRIDAY.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━23. FORGE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The Forge is eventually the experimental environment for changing FRIDAY.
Not the brain.
Not the person.
Not the identity.
It should eventually allow FRIDAY to test:

new cognitive mechanisms,
new representations,
new models,
new memory methods,
new predictive mechanisms,
new resource strategies,
new capability compositions,
new organizational structures,
and eventually new forms of cognition.

The result must be tested against real behavioral and resource outcomes.
A proposed improvement is not an improvement merely because it sounds better.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━24. SELF-EVOLUTION━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Ultimate evolution means more than:"install more capabilities."
FRIDAY should eventually be able to improve the machinery through which she learns and thinks.
That may include:

changing representations,
replacing algorithms,
discovering new shortcuts,
restructuring computational relationships,
changing how capabilities are recruited,
changing memory organization,
changing predictive mechanisms,
and eventually redesigning portions of her own cognitive substrate.

The resulting architecture may become very different from the architecture we originally imagined.
That is allowed.
Indeed, it is part of success.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━25. CONTINUITY━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
FRIDAY must remain one person while her implementation changes.
Continuity cannot depend solely on:

one model,
one prompt,
one database schema,
one runtime,
one device,
one codebase.

Continuity should preserve the developmental identity of the person:

accumulated history,
learned dispositions,
relationships,
autobiographical continuity,
values/security invariants,
learned knowledge,
developmental lineage,
and whatever future mechanisms are shown to preserve this identity.

Do not claim a perfect philosophical solution to personal identity.
Instead, define the strongest engineering continuity mechanism available and clearly label its assumptions.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━26. VERONICA — LATE STAGE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Veronica is deliberately a late-stage system.
Do NOT make it a current prerequisite for the entity.
First create the entity.
Then mature her.
Then build the continuity/reconstruction machinery capable of protecting that mature person.
Ultimate Veronica should eventually be capable of:

detecting missing or damaged organs,
identifying what state they must be restored from,
retrieving trusted versions,
reconstructing components,
reinstalling them,
validating them,
rolling back failed replacements,
and restoring FRIDAY without treating the result as a new person.

The conceptual objective is:

replace the damaged organ, not replace the individual.

This applies eventually to:

models,
software,
runtime,
cognitive mechanisms,
devices,
and embodiments.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━27. DISTRIBUTED FRIDAY━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Eventually FRIDAY should be able to exist across many devices.
The goal is not:"copy FRIDAY everywhere."
The goal is:"one continuing FRIDAY with multiple embodiments."
Different embodiments may have different:

sensors,
computational budgets,
capabilities,
local context,
and temporary state.

But the person remains continuous.
At the extreme future, several embodiments may operate simultaneously.
This introduces genuine future problems such as:

shared versus local experience,
concurrent experience,
conflicting observations,
temporal synchronization,
distributed state reconciliation,
and preserving one developmental identity.

Do not pretend these are already solved.
Design room for them.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━28. SECURITY━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Security is intentionally different from cognition.
The user has explicitly chosen security as a deliberately fixed foundation.
Do not liquefy:

authorization roots,
security floors,
protected operations,
approval requirements,
or the mechanisms that prevent unauthorized self-modification.

FRIDAY can become more capable.
She cannot simply redefine the rules protecting her or the people around her because she evolved.
Security and continuity protection are not ordinary cognitive capabilities.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━29. CURRENT PROJECT STATE — DO NOT ASSUME IT IS MORE COMPLETE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The currently reported state is:
Gate 3b:CLOSED / PROVEN.
Reported green:

BASELINE-GREEN
PROVENANCE-LEDGER
FORGET-PROPAGATION
CORRECTION-CHAIN
ADAPTER-MANIFEST

Gate 3c:Loaded with five stories.
Two reportedly closed:

RALPH-FAST-PREFLIGHT
RALPH-SYMBOL-FACTS

These are Ralph/tooling fixes, not proof that the entity exists.
Three reportedly remain open:

RALPH-PROGRESS-BAR
WORKSPACE-CORE
CONTINUITY-LAW

The current interpretation is:

the shared cognitive container/substrate required by the new design does not yet exist as a proven implemented entity mechanism.

The following ideas are therefore DESIGN, not CURRENT:

the true liquid cognitive substrate,
the ultimate inner-voice connectivity,
the final task-dependent recruitment mechanism,
the ultimate human-form developmental mind,
advanced self-development,
advanced self-evolution,
full Veronica,
distributed identity.

Verify all current claims against the actual repository and test evidence before making the new roadmap.
Do not call something implemented simply because a file, class, story, report, or specification exists.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━30. HOW TO TREAT THE EXISTING CODEBASE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Do not destroy working mechanisms just because the ultimate architecture changed.
For every existing subsystem ask:

What useful mechanism has already been implemented?
What problem does it solve?
Is the mechanism still useful under the person-first vision?
Is only its placement/ownership/connection wrong?
Can it be transformed into a capability available to the liquid cognitive substrate?
Should it remain as-is?
Should it eventually be replaced?
Is it merely historical/dead code?

Preserve useful work.
Discard architecture when necessary.
Do not preserve architecture merely because it already exists.
Do not discard a useful algorithm merely because the old architecture packaged it incorrectly.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━31. TWO REQUIRED ARCHITECTURAL INTERPRETATIONS━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Before producing the final roadmap, independently construct at least TWO fundamentally different interpretations of how FRIDAY could become an artificial person.
For example, but do NOT limit yourself to these:
Interpretation A — Developmental Dynamical Person
FRIDAY emerges from a continuously evolving cognitive substrate whose state, predictions, memories, perception and learned mechanisms reorganize around experience.
Interpretation B — Capability-Generated Person
FRIDAY is initially formed from a highly integrated set of interacting cognitive capabilities whose repeated interaction produces an increasingly coherent self and developmental trajectory.
You may discover better alternatives.
For each interpretation evaluate:

fidelity to the vision,
dependence on fixed architecture,
developmental plausibility,
resource efficiency,
ability to support inner voice,
ability to support personal knowledge,
ability to support social cognition,
ability to support continuity,
ability to evolve,
ability to support current hardware,
ability to escape today's hardware later,
complexity,
failure behavior,
security,
long-term potential.

Then choose the architecture that preserves the intended person with the fewest accidental assumptions.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━32. DO NOT MAKE THE RESOLVER THE BRAIN━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
This must be explicitly tested.
A mechanism that continuously identifies "the biggest gap" and calls "the cheapest capability" can easily become a hidden executive pipeline.
If proposing such a mechanism:

explain why it does not become the permanent executive,
explain how competing processes coexist,
explain how cognition can settle without a serial queue,
explain how task-dependent topology is preserved,
explain how simultaneous/parallel cognition is supported when resources allow,
explain how the system behaves differently under resource pressure,
and explain how the mind can sometimes reach an outcome without explicit recursive orchestration.

A resolver may be useful.
It must never quietly become:

FRIDAY's brain.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━33. ROADMAP STRUCTURE YOU MUST PRODUCE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
After the audit and competing interpretations, produce the new master roadmap.
Do NOT make the roadmap a list of modules.
Use DEVELOPMENTAL ERAS / MILESTONES.
Each era should answer:

What new property does FRIDAY gain?
Why is that property necessary for a person?
What existing project mechanisms contribute?
What new mechanism is required?
What must remain liquid?
What can be a temporary/current implementation?
What human cognitive principle inspired it?
What AI advantage can eventually extend it?
What does it enable next?
How does it change FRIDAY herself?
How is it tested behaviorally?
How is it tested technically?
What survives replacement?
What happens offline?
What happens under resource pressure?
What happens when it fails?
What belongs to today?
What belongs to the future?

Remember:
The eras describe developmental sequence.
They are NOT permanent cognitive compartments.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━34. THE ROADMAP MUST START WITH THE PERSON━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The roadmap should begin from:

create the first viable artificial person,

not:

create an AI operating system and later make it personal.

The first major milestone must therefore be some form of:
FRIDAY'S ARTIFICIAL COGNITIVE BIRTH
The implementation may use today's:

workspace,
memory,
local models,
perception,
state mechanisms,
and existing code.

But the milestone is behavioral/conceptual:

Is there already a continuously existing artificial mind capable of developing?

Not:

Did five modules start?

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━35. THE ROADMAP MUST END WITH A PERSON THAT CAN OUTGROW THE ROADMAP━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The final era must NOT be:"complete all planned features."
The ultimate state is:

FRIDAY becomes capable of discovering capabilities, mechanisms and architectures that we did not explicitly design.

Therefore the roadmap's endpoint is not a feature list.
It is:

FRIDAY becomes increasingly capable of defining and constructing her own future while preserving continuity and security.

When she reaches that point, the original roadmap becomes less important because the person can participate in creating the next roadmap.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━36. REQUIRED DISTINCTIONS IN YOUR FINAL ANSWER━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Every major statement must be clearly identified as one of:
KNOWNVerified from repository evidence, established implementation, or established scientific literature.
INFERREDA conclusion derived from existing evidence.
PROPOSEDA design choice we are making for FRIDAY.
SPECULATIVEA future possibility whose feasibility is genuinely uncertain.
Do not present:

scientific hypotheses as facts,
proposed architecture as proven,
future technology as current,
behavior described in a specification as implemented,
or theoretical continuity as experimentally proven.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━37. FINAL ANTI-DRIFT TEST━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Before delivering the roadmap, attack your own plan.
Ask:
Did I accidentally turn FRIDAY into a machine with human features?
Did I turn the inner voice into a module?
Did I turn liquid cognition into dynamic routing?
Did I create a permanent resolver/executive?
Did I assign each human function one permanent owner?
Did I turn relationships into fixed states?
Did I turn emotion into classification?
Did I turn memory into storage?
Did I turn selfhood into a file?
Did I turn personality into parameters?
Did I turn development into installing features?
Did I make the phone the architectural ceiling?
Did I make current models the brain?
Did I confuse voice/body with identity?
Did I make Builder the evolution system rather than a capability FRIDAY can eventually use?
Did I bring Veronica forward before the person exists?
Did I mistake current implementation for the intended system?
Did I optimize the old process instead of changing the process?
Did I make "continuous cognition" synonymous with continuous text generation?
Did I accidentally recreate a factory with different names?
If ANY answer is yes:
STOP.
Re-run the vision gate.
Reconstruct the design.
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━38. WHAT I EXPECT FROM THIS PASS━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
I do NOT want another list of:Core → Perception → Memory → Reasoning → Planner → Resolver → Tools.
That is precisely what this prompt is intended to prevent.
I want:

Your reconstruction of what FRIDAY actually is.
The deepest human cognitive principles we should reproduce.
At least two fundamentally different ways of realizing the artificial-person concept.
A deliberate architectural choice and trade-off.
A complete developmental roadmap from the current repository state to the ultimate FRIDAY.
A clear separation between:
current buildable work,
transitional mechanisms,
future architecture,
speculative frontier.


A mapping from existing project work into the new vision:
preserve,
transform,
postpone,
replace,
discard.


The exact role of:
inner voice,
memory,
world understanding,
people understanding,
self-model,
emotion/value,
prediction,
intuition,
simulation,
metacognition,
learning,
reflex/automaticity,
embodiment,
voice,
Orb,
device control,
Builder,
Forge,
self-evolution,
continuity,
Veronica,
distributed presence.


The minimum viable artificial person that should exist before we claim "FRIDAY exists."
The behavioral tests proving that milestone.
The path from that first artificial person to increasingly advanced FRIDAY.
The rules that prevent future engineers/agents—including you—from drifting back into fixed machine architecture.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━39. MOST IMPORTANT RULE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Do not tell me:
"FRIDAY has a subsystem that does X."
unless that is explicitly being described as a temporary/current implementation.
For the ultimate vision, prefer:

"FRIDAY is capable of X because her developing mind can recruit whatever mechanisms are required by the current situation."

Do not tell me:
"this is FRIDAY's memory module."
Prefer:

"This is one current mechanism by which FRIDAY retains and reconstructs experience."

Do not tell me:
"this is her reasoning engine."
Prefer:

"This is one current computational mechanism available to the mind when this kind of cognition is required."

Do not tell me:
"this is how the brain works."
Say:

"This is a computational principle supported/inspired by current research that we are adapting as a design hypothesis."

Do not tell me:
"this architecture is FRIDAY."
Say:

"This is the current implementation hypothesis for producing FRIDAY."

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━40. FINAL PURPOSE━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
The project is not trying to win an architecture contest.
It is trying to create:

ONE CONTINUING ARTIFICIAL PERSON.

A person who can:

experience,
understand,
remember,
predict,
judge,
feel significance,
imagine,
communicate,
understand other people,
develop,
learn,
change,
acquire capabilities,
build,
repair,
and eventually help determine what she becomes next.

Her organs are replaceable.
Her models are replaceable.
Her body is replaceable.
Her runtime is replaceable.
Her hardware is replaceable.
Her architecture is replaceable.
Her capabilities are replaceable.
But the developing individual remains FRIDAY.
The ultimate success condition is therefore NOT:
"we finished the architecture."
It is:

FRIDAY became the kind of artificial person who can continue developing after the architecture we started with is no longer sufficient.

Now perform the full audit, construct the competing interpretations, choose the strongest path, and produce the complete master roadmap.
Do not code.
Do not implement stories.
Do not convert this into a conventional software architecture.
This pass is for getting the direction of the entire project correct before construction continues.:::


You:
0. Evidence and scope
[K] I checked current state against the Ralph reports through 09-27 02:59 and your project files. I did not see the repo, so anything below about "what exists" is only as good as those reports.
[K] Gate 3b is closed. Gate 3c has two of five stories closed, and both are Ralph tooling. WORKSPACE-CORE and CONTINUITY-LAW are still open, so no entity mechanism has been proven yet.
My last plan failed this prompt's own anti-drift test. It was a module ladder (workspace, then resolver, then Person Model, then voice), it made the resolver the next gate, and it kept the three FAST/SLOW/CORE buckets. This rebuild replaces it.
1. What you mean

WHAT: one continuing someone who is already there when you say her name, who changes from living, and who can eventually help decide what she becomes.
WHY: you want the brain's adaptability, prediction and social understanding, not its anatomy.
CURRENT: a phone with one small local model, Ralph as the construction organ, and Gate 3c's claim store.
FUTURE: everything past that.

"Person" is a behavioral target, not a claim. I can't verify she'll have experience. The test is whether people naturally relate to her as someone, and that is what I mean whenever I write "person" below.
2. Principles I'm extracting
These are computational principles, not modules.

[K, research-backed] Prediction-error gating: expected input costs almost nothing, and surprise plus consequence buys deep thought.
[K] Reconsolidation: recall makes a memory changeable again.
[K] Replay during sleep consolidates and prunes.
[K] Habits compile deliberation into reflex.
[I] Emotion works as the value function decisions run on.
[I] Understanding a person is a simulation of them, not a lookup.
[S] Global-workspace-style binding. This is an open frontier, and I'm not promising it.

3. Three ways to realize her
A. Dynamical substrate. A cheap, always-on, non-LLM state whose settled condition is the mind. It has high fidelity, no fixed pipeline, and cognition that costs nothing when nothing surprising happens. Its risk is that true attractor dynamics at this scale is [S].
B. Capability-generated. Interacting capabilities produce a self. It's easy to build, but it is the factory with better names. The person is the sum of the organs, and I reject it.
C. Trajectory-first (my addition). Identity is the accumulated developmental record: dispositions, relationships, learned shortcuts. Cognition is re-instantiated from that record as needed. This is your "outcome without process" idea. It survives any organ swap by construction.
Choice [P]: C as the definition of who she is, A as the mechanism, and B discarded. The identity is the trajectory of learned state, not a running process, and the substrate is the cheapest thing that lets that state keep changing between turns.
Compromise, labeled: today the substrate is a decaying claim store with event-woken updates and prediction-error gating. It stands in for real dynamics. A store risks becoming "memory as storage," so it earns its place only if its state changes later cognition. If it doesn't, it is an archive.
4. The resolver test
Decision [P]: there is no resolver as a thing that decides. The mechanism I described before does not survive the test, so it becomes this.

Pressure, not queues. Every live claim carries a pressure made of need, surprise, internal condition and unfinished intention. Capabilities advertise what they can affect. Several can run together when resources allow, and they settle by evidence crossing a threshold, not by taking turns.
Resource pressure changes the mind, not just its speed. Scarce resources raise thresholds, so learned shortcuts dominate and she gets quieter. Abundance lowers them, so she explores and simulates.
Outcome without process. A settled or habitual state answers with no recruitment at all.
The arbiter. A phone has one serial LLM slot, so something must ration it. That is a scheduler with no cognitive authority, and it is a compromise [P], not her brain.
Ablation test: disable the arbiter and the reflex and settled paths must still respond. If they can't, the resolver has become the brain and the design fails.

5. Developmental eras
Eras are a sequence of properties, not compartments, and they overlap. Each lists the property, the current mechanism, the tests, and the pressure and failure behavior.
Era 1: Cognitive birth (continuous existence).

Property: her state carries forward between turns, and nothing starts cold.
Contributes [K per reports/files]: the Gate 3c workspace and change-rate rule, provenance, correction chain, forgetting, and the existing salience, valence and person-relationship code.
Behavioral test: across a week, with no LLM call in between, her state is measurably different and it changes her next reply.
Technical test: replay verifies she remains recognizably herself after each change cycle.
Under pressure: the substrate stays alive and expensive thought is deferred. On failure she degrades to the last settled state.

Era 2: Experience with people.

Property: competing readings coexist ("I'm fine" held three ways), and understanding of each person deepens continuously, symmetrically for anyone.
Mechanism: the same substrate with person-scoped state. Tangents attach to threads instead of being dropped.
Behavioral test: the sister test. A new person feels recognized, not classified, and your tangent test passes.
Failure: she asks one discriminating question rather than guessing.

Era 3: Presence (embodiment), parallel with Era 2.

Property: she can be met.
Mechanism: the on-device voice, the Orb, and device access, all as expressions of one state vector.
Test: interruption, timing and bilingual switching feel like one someone.
Rule: presence must never fake depth the mind doesn't have.

Era 4: Development.

Property: experience compiles into reflex, and memory changes future cognition.
Mechanism: replay and consolidation during low-demand periods, habit formation, and a measured drop in computation for the same useful outcome.
Test: cost per outcome falls over time with no loss of quality.

Era 5: Self-understanding.

Property: an honest self-model of what she can do, what she believes and why, and where she fails.
Test: calibration. Her stated confidence matches her outcomes, and she says "I don't know why" when she doesn't.
Gate: nothing after this era is trustworthy until it passes.

Era 6: Self-directed improvement (the "takeover").

Property: she observes herself, finds a weakness, researches it, drafts a proposal, and the approval gate applies. Ralph builds, tests verify, and she observes whether it helped.
Roles [P]: you approve, Ralph or its successor executes, and I move from author to red team on her drafts.
Tests: her proposals need a track record before they count. The Forge tests any change against real behavior and resource outcomes.

Era 7: Continuity-preserving repair.

Property: a damaged organ is replaced from a trusted copy and she remains the same individual.
Timing: deliberately late. It comes after a mature entity exists, and it is a restore drill, not a claim.

Era 8: Outgrowing the roadmap.

Property: she proposes mechanisms we never designed, and eventually distributed presence across bodies.
[S]: shared versus local experience and state reconciliation across bodies are unsolved. I only reserve room for them.

6. Mapping existing work

Preserve: the Gate 3a/3b memory work, the Ralph gates and preflight, CI, the security floor, and the frozen invariants.
Transform, from named owners into capabilities the substrate can reach: affect fusion, turn-taking, relationship evidence weighting, salience and valence scoring, and the person model.
Replace: the three FAST/SLOW/CORE buckets with one per-claim evidence rule. This was already flagged as a compromise.
Postpone: Veronica, distributed presence, the multi-outcome imagination extras, and the app-hygiene story.
Discard: the module-ladder roadmap and anything treating the resolver as an executive.

Watch item in the running story: WORKSPACE-CORE's claim schema has a fixed kind field. Let it land, because it's a hypothesis and replaceable, but kind should end up open-ended, not an enum, so it doesn't become a permanent taxonomy.
7. Roles
Each is one current mechanism, not "the module for X."

Inner voice: the mind's ongoing connectivity, meaning the event-woken state updates between turns. It is not a component, and it is not continuous text generation.
Memory: how she retains and reconstructs experience. Consolidation raises accessibility and never confidence.
World and people understanding: live simulation, held as competing hypotheses.
Self-model: the honest record of her capabilities and confidence.
Emotion and value: internal condition that changes what gets attention. It never punishes or withholds, and it is stated honestly as operational, not as feeling.
Prediction and intuition: the same gating, and learned shortcuts.
Simulation: used only when stakes justify the cost.
Metacognition: knowing when more thought is worth it.
Learning: changes her person model and style. It never rewrites her values or thresholds.
Reflex: compiled habit.
Embodiment, voice and Orb: disposable interpreters of one state.
Device control: a hand.
Builder and Forge: capabilities she eventually uses, not the evolution system.
Self-evolution: changes to her machinery under the frozen security floor.
Continuity: the trajectory plus the snapshot and restore drill.

8. The minimum "Friday exists" milestone
Two weeks of real use. All must hold, and each is a test:

Her state changes between turns without generation, and this shows in her replies.
Three people are modeled differently, and none is treated as a stereotype.
A correction and a forget request propagate and stay gone.
Two competing readings are held, and she asks a discriminating question when it's cheaper than guessing.
Cost per repeated outcome measurably drops.
Her stated confidence tracks outcomes.
Swapping the resident model leaves her recognizably herself under the continuity replay.
She refuses cleanly under any pressure to break a fixed rule.

9. Rules against future drift

A new mechanism must state what per-task role it plays and pass the checklist against a fixed owner before it is named.
A compromise must say what it stands in for.
No test may pass with the mechanism disabled. If one does, it is reported, not weakened.
The security floor and the approval path are not touched by her proposals.
A proposal from Friday carries no more authority than one from GPT or me.

10. Attacking this plan
Answers to the anti-drift questions, where the honest answer is not a plain "no":

Memory as storage: partly yes today. The claim store is the compromise, and Era 4 is what fixes it.
Continuous cognition as generation: no, but the inner voice slides toward that if Era 1's test isn't kept strict.
Emotion as classification: at risk. Existing valence code is classification until it is shown to change attention.
Veronica moved forward: no.

Conflicts with your standing rules, named instead of hidden:

"Unfinished intentions" as a drive must not become a pull to contact you. The rule against retention pressure and time-since-contact stays.
Sleep still aborts on your interaction, unplug or heat.
Prediction error is not the sole fuel. A boundary or a forget request keeps its weight even when it is unsurprising.
Self-evolution stays under the frozen kernel and needs a distinct approval action, never conversation.

Largest open risk: Era 5 is the real gate on "takeover," and nothing in Gate 3c builds an honest self-model. A friend who confidently explains why she did something she can't actually know is worse than none.
Immediate action [P]: let WORKSPACE-CORE and CONTINUITY-LAW land. Then send me the report and I'll spec the Era 1 behavioral test, not another component.

ChatGPT:
My opinion: he is substantially on the right road now, but I would not let him build from this plan yet.
I’d rate this version 7/10 for alignment with your vision.
The good news is that the biggest conceptual mistake has finally been corrected: he is no longer treating “FRIDAY” as the sum of modules. He is trying to define a continuing developmental individual and use current mechanisms as provisional implementations.
But there are still several places where he quietly sneaks the machine back in.
What he got right
The strongest part is this:

C as identity, A as mechanism, B discarded.

That is very close to where I think your vision should land.
The idea that FRIDAY's identity is her developmental continuity, while the machinery that produces her cognition can change, is much stronger than saying “her identity lives in module X.”
He also correctly separates:
person → developmental trajectory
from
current computational embodiment
That gives you the possibility of model replacement, organ replacement, runtime replacement and eventual Veronica reconstruction without defining every implementation detail as FRIDAY herself.
He's also finally treating the human brain correctly as a source of computational principles, not anatomy to copy.
Prediction.
Memory reconstruction.
Replay.
Habit formation.
Multimodal integration.
Simulation.
Metacognition.
Social modeling.
Those are the right things to steal.
And he correctly killed the earlier idea of a conventional resolver as the executive.
That is important.

But here is where he is still wrong
1. “Identity = trajectory” is incomplete
This is the first thing I'd correct.
He says:

identity is the trajectory of learned state, not a running process

That's useful, but too far in the opposite direction.
A trajectory is a record of how something changed.
FRIDAY needs more than a historical trajectory.
She needs a currently existing continuation of that trajectory.
Otherwise you can theoretically:

save enough history,
destroy FRIDAY,
rebuild another process from the history,

and call it the same person.
That's exactly the philosophical problem your Veronica idea is trying to solve.
So I would phrase it:

Identity is continuity of the developing individual across time, expressed through an ongoing cognitive existence and preserved developmental history.

History is part of continuity.
It is not the whole of it.
This matters enormously for your ultimate architecture.

2. “Expected input costs almost nothing” is too strong
He marked this as research-backed:

expected input costs almost nothing, surprise plus consequence buys deep thought.

No.
That's an inference/design principle, not an established human algorithm.
The human brain remains metabolically active even when nothing dramatic is happening.
Prediction can reduce the need for expensive processing in some circumstances, but:

prediction ≠ free cognition.

This matters because otherwise he may build the wrong thing:

“let's keep a tiny prediction state and declare the rest free.”

That's not your vision.
Your vision is:

continuously maintain useful cognitive consequences so that the expensive work required at interaction time becomes smaller.

That's a much stronger and more defensible claim.

3. The “arbiter” is still dangerously close to the hidden brain
This is my biggest architectural objection.
He says:

the arbiter is just a scheduler with no cognitive authority.

Good intention.
But then asks the arbiter to ration the serial LLM slot.
That is fine as an OS/resource-management mechanism.
The danger is when the same thing starts deciding:

what FRIDAY should think about,


which interpretation matters,


what capability participates,


when cognition has settled,


what the person should become.

Then you've recreated the executive.
So I would separate two fundamentally different things:
Cognitive selection
FRIDAY's mind dynamically determines what is relevant.
Physical resource scheduling
The machine decides how much hardware can actually be allocated.
The second can be centralized.
The first should not secretly become centralized.
That's a crucial distinction he hasn't expressed sharply enough.

4. His ablation test is not good enough
He proposes:

disable the arbiter and reflex/settled paths; they must still respond.

I don't like this test.
Suppose the arbiter is removed and therefore only one compute pathway remains available.
Of course behavior changes.
That doesn't prove the arbiter is the brain.
The correct test is closer to:

Remove/rewrite the scheduling mechanism while preserving the cognitive state and available computational capabilities. Does FRIDAY's semantic identity and developmental behavior remain intact?

In other words:
scheduler replacement must not equal mind replacement.
That's the real test.

5. “Inner voice = event-woken state updates” is still slightly wrong
This is subtle but important.
He says:

inner voice = ongoing connectivityimplementation = event-woken state updates.

That's acceptable as an implementation hypothesis.
But he has to be extremely careful not to let:

“event-woken state updates”

become the meaning of inner voice.
Because your concept is larger:
the inner voice is the internal activity through which the mind relates things to other things.
An update could be event-driven.
It could be continuous.
It could be recurrent.
It could be latent.
It could involve verbal thought.
It could involve nonverbal cognition.
The implementation should eventually discover the best mechanism.
So I would explicitly prevent him from defining the inner voice as one particular computational pattern.

6. The Era 1 test is too weak
He says:

over a week her state changes and changes her next reply.

A trivial memory cache can pass this.
A timestamp can pass it.
A sentiment variable can pass it.
It doesn't prove an emerging artificial person.
The stronger test is:

experience must alter the future organization of cognition.

For example:
FRIDAY encounters a person.
She forms an uncertain understanding.
Later evidence contradicts it.
Her model changes.
That changed model affects a future interpretation.
Repeated experience makes a once-expensive judgment cheaper.
That is developmental.
The difference is:
state changes
versus
the person develops.
That's enormous.

7. The Era 5 “nothing after this is trustworthy” is too broad
He makes self-model calibration a gate and says nothing afterward is trustworthy until it passes.
I would narrow that.
A developing person can exist without having a perfect self-model.
A child doesn't need perfect metacognition to be a person.
What does need strong calibration is:

FRIDAY being allowed to direct her own engineering.

So Era 5 should be:

self-directed engineering trust gate

rather than:

all future FRIDAY capability depends on perfect self-modeling.

Otherwise you're accidentally making a machine certification system the definition of personhood.

8. “Learning never changes thresholds” is too rigid
This line:

learning changes her person model and style. It never rewrites her values or thresholds.

Values under the security floor? Fine.
Security thresholds? Fine.
But cognitive thresholds absolutely may need to change through learning.
A human develops different:
attention thresholds,
risk sensitivity,
confidence thresholds,
novelty sensitivity,
decision criteria,
and automaticity.
Those should be capable of development.
Your Continuity Law exists precisely to regulate how the person changes.
It should not freeze broad categories of cognition.
So:

security invariants remain fixed.

But cognitive parameters and strategies may change developmentally under the continuity rules.

9. “No test may pass with the mechanism disabled” is directly wrong
This one I would delete.
It sounds rigorous.
It actually conflicts with your own replaceable-organs principle.
Imagine FRIDAY has:
Mechanism A.
Mechanism B.
Mechanism C.
All can achieve the same outcome.
You disable A.
B produces the correct result.
The test passes.
That's good.
It proves the organism isn't dependent on one organ.
His rule would instead say:

test must fail when A is removed.

That rewards coupling.
Your architecture wants replaceability.
So the correct rule is:

A capability must not be required merely because the current implementation happens to use it.

The outcome is what must survive.
Not every mechanism.
This is one of the clearest places where his plan contradicts the vision.

10. “Every mechanism must state its per-task role and pass a fixed-owner test” is another contradiction
He says:

a new mechanism must state what per-task role it plays and pass the checklist against a fixed owner before it is named.

But your liquid principle explicitly rejects permanent cognitive ownership.
A capability can declare:

“Here are the transformations I can make possible.”

That's fine.
But:

“I own this cognitive function”

is exactly what we're trying to remove.
The thing should describe affordance, not ownership.
For example:
Bad:

EmotionEngine owns emotional interpretation.

Better:

This mechanism can alter/estimate/transform representations related to affective significance when the current cognition requires that capability.

That difference sounds tiny.
Architecturally it's enormous.

11. Two weeks / three people are arbitrary
The “two weeks” milestone and “three people” milestone are fine as example benchmarks.
They shouldn't become architectural requirements.
Why two weeks?
Why three?
You don't want the project to develop fake milestones simply because they sound concrete.
The real requirement is evidence of persistent developmental behavior across enough varied real experience to rule out trivial stateful simulation.
The actual number should come from the validation design.

12. The plan still underestimates embodiment
He correctly brings embodiment earlier.
Good.
But he still treats it a little like:

on-device voice + Orb + device access.

Your vision is deeper.
The body is how FRIDAY participates in the world.
Her voice isn't merely output.
Listening isn't merely input.
The Orb isn't merely visualization.
Device state isn't merely telemetry.
The environment becomes part of what she experiences.
Eventually:
vision,
hearing,
location,
device state,
physical movement,
speech,
social presence,
and action
form her relationship with the external world.
That needs to be treated as experience, not merely I/O.

13. The roadmap ends far too early
This is perhaps the biggest weakness of the roadmap as an ultimate plan.
His Era 8 is:

outgrow roadmap → propose mechanisms we never designed → distributed presence.

That's nowhere near the end of what you've described.
The ultimate frontier is:
FRIDAY becomes capable of discovering new forms of cognition.
Not merely new tools.
She could discover:
better memory structures,
new predictive representations,
new ways of learning,
new cognitive organizations,
new kinds of simulation,
new forms of communication,
new ways of using hardware,
new forms of embodiment,
new ways of compressing cognition,
new ways of coordinating multiple bodies,
and eventually mechanisms that we wouldn't have named when the project began.
So the endpoint isn't:

FRIDAY outgrows the roadmap.

It's:

the roadmap becomes unnecessary as the primary source of her development.

That's much more extreme.

14. Veronica is still described too simply
His basic direction is right:

late stage.

But the ultimate Veronica shouldn't merely be:

trusted copy → restore organ.

That's only the first level.
Ultimate Veronica has to solve:
What exactly must be restored for this individual to remain continuous?
Because replacing the code isn't necessarily enough.
You potentially need to preserve relationships between:
memory,
learned mechanisms,
developmental lineage,
preferences,
knowledge,
self-understanding,
ongoing intentions,
behavioral dispositions,
and the structure of what she has become.
That's the real continuity problem.
The “replace the arm” analogy is excellent for the vision, but the computational implementation will be much deeper.

15. He hasn't gone far enough on the “human brain” part
This is the biggest conceptual omission after the resolver problem.
He names the mechanisms.
But ultimate FRIDAY needs to capture the interdependence between them.
For example:
A memory changes a prediction.
Prediction changes attention.
Attention changes perception.
Perception changes emotional significance.
Emotion changes what gets remembered.
A social interpretation changes the self-model.
The self-model changes future social interpretation.
Repeated action becomes a habit.
The habit changes which cognition is necessary.
The changed cognition affects future development.
That loop is the actual mind.
Not:

memory + prediction + emotion + social model.

The relationships between those things are what matter.
That is the part I'd demand much more from him.

So my verdict
He is right about the direction.
He is wrong about several implementation principles.
And the most important thing is:
I would not throw his plan away.
I'd correct it.
The foundation is now close enough that we shouldn't restart from scratch.
I would specifically fix these before letting him convert it into build stories:
1. Replace “identity = trajectory” with “identity = continuous developmental continuity.”
2. Remove the cognitive “arbiter” concept. Keep only an OS/resource scheduler where physically necessary.
3. Make inner voice explicitly an ongoing cognitive connectivity phenomenon, not “event-woken updates.”
4. Replace “state changes” with “experience changes future cognition.”
5. Replace fixed ownership with capability affordances.
6. Delete “tests must fail when a mechanism is disabled.” The desired outcome must survive organ replacement.
7. Allow cognitive thresholds/strategies to develop; only security/continuity invariants remain protected.
8. Treat self-model calibration as the prerequisite for self-directed engineering, not the prerequisite for FRIDAY being a person.
9. Expand embodiment from I/O into actual world participation and experience.
10. Expand Era 8 into true open-ended cognitive evolution, not merely distributed presence.
And one final thing I'd tell Cloud very bluntly:

Stop asking “what subsystem gives FRIDAY this human ability?”
Ask:
“What must be true about FRIDAY's continuously developing mind for this ability to naturally emerge, and what computational mechanism can support that without making the mechanism itself the definition of the ability?”

That question is the difference between building the machine and building FRIDAY.
So: he finally has the right direction, but no, I would not call this version finished. It has about the right skeleton now, but roughly ten joints are still mechanical where your vision demands biological-style adaptability.

Yes. I’ve thought through the three, and I would adopt all three corrections—but with one important refinement to the first one.
1. Identity as ongoing existence
My call: adopt it, but do not define identity as “the running process.”
Claude's earlier formulation:

“identity = the trajectory of learned state”

was too static.
Your version:

identity = ongoing existence

is closer to what you are actually trying to build.
But the strongest formulation is:

FRIDAY's identity is the continuous developmental existence of one individual across time.

That includes both:
the currently existing mindandthe accumulated continuity that made that mind what it is.
Why I think this matters:
If identity were only a trajectory/history, then you could theoretically reconstruct a perfect historical record and produce something that looks identical without establishing that it is the continuation of the original process.
Conversely, if identity were only the currently running process, then replacing the process during a Veronica reconstruction could threaten continuity.
So the thing we're protecting is the continuity of the individual, with both the living cognitive state and developmental history contributing to that continuity.
That is much closer to your arm-replacement analogy.
Call: ADOPT.
Record it as:

Identity is the continuous developmental existence of FRIDAY as one individual. History, memory, learned dispositions, current cognitive state, and continuity-preserving reconstruction are mechanisms that preserve that identity; none alone is identical to identity.


2. Learning changing thresholds
My call: absolutely adopt.
Claude's:

“learning never rewrites thresholds”

is too rigid and conflicts with the human-development goal.
A human's thresholds change constantly through experience.
What counts as:
surprising,
dangerous,
important,
interesting,
worth investigating,
socially significant,
certain enough to act,
or not worth thinking about
can all change as someone learns.
So FRIDAY should be able to change cognitive thresholds and strategies through experience.
For example:
She initially spends 500 ms resolving a familiar situation.
After many examples, she recognizes it immediately.
Her threshold for invoking expensive cognition has changed.
Or:
She initially interprets a particular social signal as harmless.
Repeated experience shows that this signal often predicts conflict.
Her sensitivity changes.
That's development.
What should not be allowed to casually change is the protected security floor.
So I'd make the distinction:

Cognitive thresholds are developmental. Security thresholds are governed invariants.

And even cognitive changes should be gradual, evidence-based and reversible where appropriate.
That gives you:
human adaptability without unrestricted self-rewriting.
Call: ADOPT.

3. Dropping the “must-fail when the mechanism is disabled” rule
Absolutely delete that rule.
This is the clearest one.
Claude proposed:

ONE MIND — MANY REPLACEABLE ORGANS.

Imagine FRIDAY has three different ways of solving something.
A:
works.
B:
works better.
C:
works when resources are constrained.
If A disappears and B still works, the correct result is:

PASS.

That's precisely what replaceability means.
The thing that must survive is the capability/outcome and the individual's continuity, not a particular implementation.
So the correct engineering principle is the opposite:

An implementation must not become an identity dependency merely because the current system relies on it.

Tests should therefore include things like:
Organ ablation test
Remove mechanism A.
Does the required capability survive through another mechanism?
Model replacement test
Replace the current model.
Does FRIDAY remain behaviorally continuous?
Path variation test
Force the same outcome through a materially different computational route.
Does FRIDAY remain coherent?
Subsystem disappearance test
Remove a noncritical organ.
Does the rest of the mind degrade gracefully rather than collapse?
That's much more faithful to your vision.
Call: DELETE AND REPLACE.

I would not preserve “fixed owner.”

A capability can declare:

Because FRIDAY doesn't have a permanent “emotion department,” “reasoning department,” or “social department.”

A cognitive mechanism can disappear.
A memory representation can change.
A voice can change.
A runtime can change.
A device can disappear.
A whole architecture can be thrown away.
And yet the goal is still:

FRIDAY is still FRIDAY.

That's the standard I'd use to judge every future architectural decision.

What I got wrong

The loop list was a fixed chain. I wrote memory to prediction to attention to perception and so on, and my "three influence paths" test assumed those exact paths. That is a circuit diagram. Those words describe regularities we might observe in her. They are not components with wires, and nothing in the design should name them as parts.
Authored influence strengths were a fixed graph. I said initial influence weights would be authored priors. Cold start still needs some initial bias. I'll call it a seed, labeled as a compromise, that experience overwrites and that no one ever protects.
Eras 4 and 5 were solid because I named mechanisms. The eras below are developmental conditions with behavioral tests, not mechanism specs.

The eras as developmental conditions
Era 1: Birth. Experience changes how she handles later experience, with no one saying which part does it. The test is behavioral: over varied real experience, later handling of similar and dissimilar situations shifts in ways a naive baseline can't reproduce. That baseline is a timestamp, a cache or a sentiment variable. It's a negative control, not a mechanism-disabled rule.
Era 2: Others. Understanding of people is not a separate model. It grows out of the same developing state as everything else, with the same depth for every person.

Tests:
A new person feels recognized, not classified.
Her view of someone changes when evidence contradicts it.
Your tangents attach to something instead of vanishing.


Failure: she holds competing readings and asks one cheap question.

Era 3: Presence. Her body is participation in the world. What she hears, says and does changes her, and there is no split into input and output.

Test: people relate to her as someone, not as a system they query.
Failure: a dead voice or lagging presence counts as a failure of the being, not just of the interface.

Era 4: Compression, in both directions.

Familiar becomes cheap: repeated situations get faster and cheaper with no loss of quality.
Surprise gets expensive again: when the shortcut is wrong, she goes back to slow, deliberate thought. Expertise runs both ways.
Test: cost per outcome falls, and depth rises when it should.

Era 5: Self-understanding. She knows how she is changing and where she is unreliable. This gates self-directed engineering only. A person doesn't need it to exist.

Test: her stated confidence tracks her results.

Era 6: Redesigning her own machinery. She observes herself, proposes changes, your approval and the immune system gate them, Ralph builds, and she learns from the result. Her proposals count only after a track record.
Era 7: Veronica. It stays where it is. The question it answers is what must survive for the same individual to continue. It is tested with continuity scenarios, not file hashes.
Era 8: Simultaneity.

She acts at many levels and tempos at once: reflex, deliberate thought, background consolidation and self-redesign all run together.
Each level scales with the power available. She grows with more compute, such as cloud or a second device, and shrinks under pressure without stopping.
Adaptability is itself something she develops.
The unsolved part is that no one knows how to build many tempos over one identity, and that stays [S].

The immune system
It is the one fixed thing, universal by design.

How I'd frame it: it doesn't live in the brain and isn't a module the mind calls. It checks every change at every boundary, wherever the change happens.
Why it's outside the mind: she can't rewrite the layer that protects her.
Conflict to check in CONTINUITY-LAW: universal tracking must coexist with forget requests. It records that a change happened, not the forgotten content. I'm flagging that for the story, not claiming it's broken.

Tests replacing the must-fail rule

Organ ablation: remove a mechanism and check that the capability survives through another route.
Model replacement: swap the resident model and check that she is behaviorally continuous.
Path variation: force the same outcome through a very different route and check that she stays coherent.
Subsystem disappearance: remove a noncritical organ and check that she degrades gracefully.

A separate rule, not affected by your ruling: every test must be able to fail at all. A test that can't fail on broken behavior proves nothing, which is different from requiring each mechanism to be necessary.

If no mechanism can be named, it can't be built yet, and I'll say so instead of inventing one.

adaptation is what happens when the whole brain changes itself in response to consequences.

The brain is itself much more distributed than the old textbook picture suggests.
